=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two strings s and pattern.
A string x is called almost equal to y if you can change at most one character in x to make it identical to y.
Return the smallest starting index of a substring in s that is almost equal to pattern. If no such index exists, return -1.
A substring is a contiguous non-empty sequence of characters within a string.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "abcdefg", pattern = "bcdffg"
Output: 1
Explanation:
The substring s[1..6] == "bcdefg" can be converted to "bcdffg" by changing s[4] to "f".

Example 2:

Input: s = "ababbababa", pattern = "bacaba"
Output: 4
Explanation:
The substring s[4..9] == "bababa" can be converted to "bacaba" by changing s[6] to "c".

Example 3:

Input: s = "abcd", pattern = "dba"
Output: -1

Example 4:

Input: s = "dde", pattern = "d"
Output: 0

 
Constraints:

1 <= pattern.length < s.length <= 10^5
s and pattern consist only of lowercase English letters.

 
Follow-up: Could you solve the problem if at most k consecutive characters can be changed?

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minStartingIndex(self, s: str, pattern: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

for i in range(1,n):

    if i+z[i]-1 > r:

return z

Pseudo:

class Solution:
    def minStartingIndex(self, s: str, pattern: str) -> int:
        n, m = len(s), len(pattern)
        sep = '#'
        z1 = self._z(pattern + sep + s)
        rp = pattern[::-1]
        rs = s[::-1]
        z2 = self._z(rp + sep + rs)
        base = m + 1
        for i in range(n - m + 1): # Wait pattern.length < s.length, but starting index substring length m must i <= n-m. Need check. Example pattern length < s.length, but substring length m can start at n-m. Need loop range(n-m+1). I earlier loop n maybe if i+m > n invalid. Need restrict. Example n=3,m=1 range3. Good.
            a = z1[base + i]
            if a >= m:
                return i
            e = i + m - 1
            need = m - a - 1
            # if need <= 0 return i? If a=m-1 need=0, z2 >=0 always. Could just check.
            if z2[base + (n - 1 - e)] >= need:
                return i
        return -1

Let's write code:

class Solution:
    def minStartingIndex(self, s: str, pattern: str) -> int:
        def z_function(t: str):
            N = len(t)
            z = [0] * N
            l = r = 0
            for i in range(1, N):
                if i <= r:
                    zi = min(r - i + 1, z[i - l])
                else:
                    zi = 0
                while i + zi < N and t[zi] == t[i + zi]:
                    zi += 1
                z[i] = zi
                if i + zi - 1 > r:
                    l = i
                    r = i + zi - 1
            return z

        n, m = len(s), len(pattern)
        sep = '#'
        z1 = z_function(pattern + sep + s)
        z2 = z_function(pattern[::-1] + sep + s[::-1])
        base = m + 1
        for i in range(n - m + 1):
            pref = z1[base + i]
            if pref >= m:
                return i
            end = i + m - 1
            need = m - pref - 1
            if z2[base + (n - 1 - end)] >= need:
                return i
        return -1

Need think if z2[base + (n - 1 - end)] when need=0 always valid; yes. But what if pref maybe >m? Not possible but if pref=m? handled. If pref=m-1, need=0, z2 check >=0 true. Good.

For start i:
pref = LCP(s[i:], pattern) = max p such that s[i+t] = pattern[t] for t<p.
If pref=m, exact.
Mismatch at t=pref.
Need to check s[i+pref+1 : i+m] == pattern[pref+1 : m].
Let L = m-pref-1.
This equality is equivalent to for r=1..L: s[i+m-r] == pattern[m-r].
The left side is the last L chars of s[0 : i+m] (prefix ending at end). The right side is last L chars of pattern. z2 at end = i+m-1 gives max L such that for r=1..L: s[end-r+1] == pattern[m-r]. So if z2 >= L, equality. Good.

Now, z2 computation:
Let rs = reverse(s), rp = reverse(pattern). For end e, pos = n-1-e. Then rs[pos] = s[e], rs[pos+1] = s[e-1], etc. rp[0] = pattern[m-1], rp[1] = pattern[m-2]. LCP(rs[pos:], rp) = max L such that for q=0..L-1: s[e-q] == pattern[m-1-q]. This is same. Good.

Now, z2 combined = rp + sep + rs. z at base+pos = LCP(rs[pos:], rp). Good.

Let's consider if there is a possible issue with "almost equal" if at most one character
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def minStartingIndex(self, s: str, pattern: str) -> int:
        def z_function(t: str):
            n = len(t)
            z = [0] * n
            l = r = 0
            for i in range(1, n):
                if i <= r:
                    z[i] = min(r - i + 1, z[i - l])
                while i + z[i] < n and t[z[i]] == t[i + z[i]]:
                    z[i] += 1
                if i + z[i] - 1 > r:
                    l, r = i, i + z[i] - 1
            return z

        n, m = len(s), len(pattern)
        sep = '#'

        # z1[base + i] = LCP(s[i:], pattern)
        z1 = z_function(pattern + sep + s)

        # z2[base + pos] = LCP(reverse(s)[pos:], reverse(pattern))
        z2 = z_function(pattern[::-1] + sep + s[::-1])

        base = m + 1

        for i in range(n - m + 1):
            pref = z1[base + i]
            if pref >= m:
                return i

            end = i + m - 1
            need = m - pref - 1

            # Check whether the part after the first mismatch matches.
            if z2[base + (n - 1 - end)] >= need:
                return i

        return -1
```
